# Decision log

Routine decisions made inside an approved phase, one line each with the reason. Newest last.

## Phase 0, October 20, 2026

| # | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D0-01 | Phase 0 runs in this cloud session, in `oath-and-action-v6-brand-guide/` at the repository root | Dustin asked to begin with the first prompt here. This session cannot reach the Cowork folder |
| D0-02 | The seven studio agents run as general-purpose subagents, each briefed to read and adopt its role file in `.claude/agents/` | This session's agent roster was fixed when it started, so it cannot load project agents from a subfolder. The role files still govern each pass |
| D0-03 | The main session did the engineer's Phase 0 work (extraction, rendering, census, axe scan) | The audits depended on these outputs, so they ran first and in sequence |
| D0-04 | Files under `work/extract/` keep short slug names, and supplied reference files keep their supplied names | Extracts are generated data addressed by scripts. Supplied names keep each file traceable to its source |
| D0-05 | `.gitignore` excludes the large supplied originals, `work/extract/`, raw screenshots and `node_modules/` | All of them can be regenerated by the scripts. The source manifest records checksums |
| D0-06 | axe-core 4.10.2 installed locally in `work/build/` | Needed for the automated accessibility scan. It is not committed |
| D0-07 | Ledger and gate report filenames now carry a date prefix in both copies of the kit | The undated names in the first version of the kit broke Dustin's naming rule |
| D0-08 | Screenshots are saved as JPEG at quality 72, then tiled at 1,700 px for review | Keeps 70 full-chapter captures under 40 MB and legible when an agent views them |
| D0-09 | Text census excludes text inside SVG artwork | Diagram text is drawn at artwork scale and needs a separate check in Phase 3 |

## Dustin's decisions, October 20, 2026 (before Gate 0)

| # | Question | Decision | Effect on later phases |
|---|---|---|---|
| DC-01 | How far can an Act piece go? | Labeled positions allowed. Act may ask readers to support a specific measure when the piece is labeled as opinion or an editorial position | Phase 1 strategy defines the label, where it sits, and how process-only Act pieces differ from labeled position pieces. Examples still rotate across issues |
| DC-02 | May the studio redraw the four pillar marks? | Yes, from one shared skeleton per pillar, with filled and line versions | Phase 1 proposes the skeletons and up to two directions. Nothing changes before Gate 1 approval |
| DC-03 | Should the atlas use the guide's fonts? | Converge on Inter Tight, Inter and Pillar Red | The atlas applications drop the renamed URW Nimbus faces and V7's darker red |
| DC-04 | Status of the V5 voice line | Editable. The editor may propose a revision beside the original at Gate 1. The original stays in force until approved | Phase 1 editor task |

## Gate 0, approved by Dustin on October 21, 2026

Dustin approved the review, the Master Brief corrections (issued as Brief V2) and the Phase 1 proposal.

| # | Question | Decision | Effect on later phases |
|---|---|---|---|
| DC-05 | What is the brand's central idea? | The ampersand is the hinge: a promise or commitment, then action. The ampersand runs through the brand and joins pairs such as problem and solution, cause and effect, you and me, and people finding common ground across the left and the right | Phase 1 strategy builds the brand narrative, the hinge system and the pairing rules on it. Pairings stay nonpartisan in form and color |
| DC-06 | Audience | National. Massachusetts examples may appear, but the system serves a national audience | Examples, data and workflows default to national and multi-state material |
| DC-07 | Platforms at launch | Substack, email, X, LinkedIn, Instagram-sized posts, link previews and TikTok | Phase 4 adds TikTok formats |
| DC-08 | Atlas V7 inconsistencies | Prepare them for an atlas V7.1 | The data visualization lead writes an issue list for the atlas editor. V6 still never edits the model |
| DC-09 | Research access | Dustin wants the best output and will grant more access when asked | Network access for page fetches was requested on October 21. Until it changes, research rests on official-domain search results and flags unread quotes |
| DC-10 | References | Dustin shared `source/pics_i_like.pdf` (54 pages of brand mockups and concepts) and four mockups in `source/brand-mockups/` | Phase 1 studies them for principles. Several carry the lines "From spectators to stewards." and "Democracy has a structure.", which go to Gate 1 as candidate lines beside the locked lines |

## Phase 1, October 21, 2026

| # | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D1-01 | Master Brief V2 becomes the governing brief. V1 stays in `brief/` for the record, and `CLAUDE.md` and the agent files in this project now point to V2 | Gate 0 approved the corrections |
| D1-02 | New files in Phase 1 carry the date 20261021 | The execution date changed |
| D1-03 | The prompt pack in `oath-and-action-v6-brand-guide-prompt/` keeps Brief V1 | It is the reusable template. Brief V2 is specific to this project |
| D1-04 | Derived colors are mixed in OKLab with `color-mix`, toward Charcoal or Newsprint | OKLCH mixes toward Newsprint swing the hue, for example the error band would turn beige. OKLab matches OKLCH toward Charcoal |
| D1-05 | Phase 1 Python scripts load the shared `oa_color.py` by file path | Running Python with `-I` drops the script folder from the import path |
| D1-06 | The draft token JSON uses the `org.oathandaction` namespace | A stable reverse-domain namespace for token extensions. Ownership of oathandaction.org is unconfirmed, so the namespace can change before Phase 5 |
| D1-07 | The shared board stylesheet sets tags at 13 px | The UX lead found them at 12 px, under the 13 px floor |
| D1-08 | In the wireframes, the masthead offset sits on the content as `scroll-margin-top` | Anchored headings land clear of the sticky masthead without script |
| D1-09 | Failure specimens in wireframes are drawn as images with alt text | A failure example must not add failing text to the page's own accessibility results |
| D1-10 | Board renders use the font files extracted from V5 Polished | This environment cannot load Google Fonts, so renders would otherwise fall back to system faces |
| D1-11 | The Phase 2 and Phase 4 prompt chapter lists will follow the architecture map approved at Gate 1 | The map renumbers chapters into six parts and 28 chapters |
| D1-12 | Board copy uses American spelling | Matches the brief and a national audience |
| D1-13 | Hinge gap spacing is measured as white area 0.15x deep, with a 0.07x floor | Equal white area reads as equal gaps, where equal nearest distance does not |
| D1-14 | Ampersand option B carries extra weight so its stem reaches 0.85 of the wordmark stem | Keeps the drawn ampersand from reading lighter than the letters, the main Phase 0 finding |
| D1-15 | skia-pathops and shapely installed to user site-packages for boolean path geometry | Needed to merge and measure outlines. Not part of any shipped file |
| D1-16 | Symbol similarity limit: two symbols are distinct when their blurred correlation is under 0.60 | A studio threshold, to be confirmed with reader tests |
| D1-17 | A 16 or 24 px master passes pixel fit when at least 60 percent of its ink pixels are crisp | A studio threshold set from the Phase 0 measurements |
| D1-18 | Patterns cover no more than 12 percent of an area that carries text | Keeps texture behind text from lowering legibility. A studio threshold |
| D1-19 | Correction to D1-10: Google Fonts load in this environment (checked October 21); other outside sites stay blocked. Board renders may use either the Google Fonts files or the V5 font files, which are the same families and versions | The creative director confirmed the fonts load; a direct check returned HTTP 200 for fonts.googleapis.com and failures for every other site tried |
| D1-20 | The creative director's red rule governs every board: signature red (wordmark ampersand, lockmark lever) does not count; each view carries one working red by precedence (error, then the step a reader can take now, then the point the headline names); the hinge divider's ampersand is Charcoal; the Act handle is red only when the Act mark is the call to act; red never sits on Prussian | Five boards described red's job five ways. One rule goes to Dustin at Gate 1 as decision I10 |
| D1-21 | No brand graphic uses a dotted or dashed line | Atlas V7 gives dotted and dashed lines to evidence grades, so a dotted hinge would read as "hypothesis" |
| D1-22 | The hinge divider's ampersand takes the ink of its surface: Charcoal on Newsprint and White, Newsprint on Charcoal, White on Prussian | The red rule makes it Charcoal, which would vanish on dark surfaces. White on Prussian matches treatment D for identity marks |
| D1-23 | The ask button uses a Pillar Red fill with White labels at 19 px bold or larger (4.17:1, large text only) and a Charcoal keyline on Newsprint. On Prussian it uses a Newsprint fill with a Charcoal label | It is the step a reader can take now, second in the red rule's precedence, and red never sits on Prussian |
| D1-24 | The 37 Gate 1 decisions go to Dustin in three groups: seven big calls he answers one by one, six identity fixes he approves by name, and 22 studio recommendations he approves as a set | Every identity change still gets a named approval (CLAUDE.md gate rule 4), and 37 separate questions would bury the seven that change the brand |
| D1-25 | The combined Gate 1 board is built by script, and its decision and rubric tables are read from the Gate 1 report | The board and the report cannot disagree, as at Gate 0 |
| D1-26 | The red rule's wording for Gate 1, superseding D1-20's wording, quoted exactly on every board: "The wordmark's ampersand and the lockmark's lever are signature red and do not count, and neither does the ampersand inside the hinge diagram, which is an identity mark. The hinge diagram never shares a view with the wordmark and never stands in for the hinge divider. Each view (one screen, card, slide or printed page) then carries one working red, in this order of precedence: an error the reader must fix; the step a reader can take now; the one point the headline names, which in a systems figure is where a reform acts. One job may take several marks, such as each target of one reform. A hinge headline's ampersand uses the view's working red. The hinge divider's ampersand takes its surface's ink: Charcoal on Newsprint and White, Newsprint on Charcoal, White on Prussian. The Act handle is red only when the Act mark is itself the call to act, as on a social tile. In the guide itself, specimens, labeled failure cases and palette swatches do not count. Red never sits on Prussian." | The creative director's re-check found four points the first wording left open: the divider's ink (D1-22), the hinge diagram's ampersand, several marks for one reform, and the guide's own failure cases and swatches |
| D1-27 | The red rule's ID is Rule 05.3, in 05 Color, where the map, wireframes and shell already place it. Rule 01.1 in 01 Purpose becomes "Red never marks a party, a side or a person.", restored from NP-04. Rules 01.2 to 01.20 keep their numbers and words | One rule, one ID. The party clause fell out when the red rule replaced NP-04 |

| D2-01 | Rule 05.3 now reads, word for word: "The wordmark's ampersand and the lockmark's lever are signature red and do not count, and neither do the ampersands inside the hinge diagram and the hinge-circle badge, which are identity marks. The hinge diagram never shares a view with the wordmark and never stands in for the hinge divider. An icon's red accent counts as signature red when the icon stands alone, as an app tile, an avatar or a single feature icon. In a row or a menu, icons go one color and only the current one keeps its red. Each view (one screen, card, slide or printed page) then carries one working red, in this order of precedence: an error the reader must fix; the step a reader can take now; the one point the headline names, which in a systems figure is where a reform acts. One job may take several marks, such as each target of one reform. A hinge headline's ampersand uses the view's working red. The hinge divider's ampersand takes its surface's ink: Charcoal on Newsprint and White, Newsprint on Charcoal, White on Prussian. In the guide itself, specimens, labeled failure cases and palette swatches do not count. ## Direction review answers, October 29, 2026

Dustin sent the Civic Line catalog, 122 icons on five sheets in red-black and black, and asked the studio to reconstruct it as the icon set. The maps and the rest of the direction review wait until the icons are final. His answers to the studio's five questions follow.

| # | Decision | Source |
|---|---|---|
| DR-01 | Civic Line is the icon family. It replaces Overprint and Moving part, which closes Choice 2 of the direction review. P1 and P2 fall away, since both concerned Overprint drawings. The Overprint and Moving part files stay where they are | Dustin, October 29: "yes civic line is the winner. please proceed." |
| DR-02 | Named exception to the Master Brief's rights rule for references (section 2, "Never trace, copy, embed or ship them"): the studio traces the ten Civic Line sheets into vector masters. The exception covers these sheets only | Dustin, October 29: "i grant the exception. please trace." |
| DR-03 | The tracing cleans each drawing to its evident intent: one measured line weight across the set, true circles and straight lines where the drawing plainly means them, and every feature, count, gap and red placement kept as drawn. Each correction is logged by icon ID | Dustin, October 29: "i want my logos to look exactly like these, but cleaner, more polished" and "clean them up as needed." |
| DR-04 | Named approval of a change to identity geometry: Civic Line's Explain (CL-019), Track (CL-020), Connect (CL-021) and Act (CL-022) replace the Gate 1 pillar marks (Direction A, Y1). The side by side at actual size goes in the delivery report for the record | Dustin, October 29: "use these new pillar marks." |
| DR-05 | The Civic Line contact sheets carry the approved Edition 06 wordmark master in place of the wordmark drawn on the reference sheets | Dustin, October 29: "use approved edition 06" |
| DR-06 | Order of work: the Civic Line icons first, then the visual system for the atlas and systems maps, then the other open items of the direction review (Choice 1, Choice 3, P3, R1 to R14, Q-13) | Dustin, October 29 |

### Civic Line pipeline, October 29, 2026

| # | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D2-31 | One line weight for the whole Civic Line set: W = 5.5 source px, the median of the per-icon medians (5.55), read at 8x | DR-03; the sheets range from 4.75 to 6.0 px by accident of generation |
| D2-32 | One uniform scale for the set, s = 0.487395 viewBox units per source px, so the widest icon (R-44, 238 px) fills 116 of 128 units and strokes export at 2.68 units; each icon's ink-box center maps to (64, 64) | Keeps the source's relative sizes and one stroke across the set. Tracers draw in crop pixels, so the export scale can change later without retracing |
| D2-33 | Where a stroke changes from red to black, the red ends with a square cut and the black continues | Matches the sheets, where the color change has no cap |
| D2-34 | Six tracers work in parallel on about 20 icons each, with one status file per icon and the completion ledger compiled from them; an independent reviewer marks icons reviewed | Parallel work without write conflicts; an interrupted run resumes from the status files |
| D2-35 | Independent review of all 122 traces: 100 pass and 22 go back for fixes (13 with a must fix). The reviewer's calls stand: CL-007's marks return to W; R-02's lighter frames and numerals and R-10's heavier torso keep their weights, since at W the counters fill and the walker becomes a stick figure; CL-016's stray teardrop is deleted; CL-018 stays a shield; CL-046's chip becomes a true square; R-20's hairline stays out; R-22's centring and R-04's district lines at W are confirmed | `work/handoffs/phase-2/20261029_Civic-Line-Review.md` |

This overturns the studio's first one-size instruction | The sheet draws two seat types; centreline spacing keeps the end seats within about 2.7 px of the sheet |

## Civic Line variants, October 29, 2026

Dustin confirmed the Civic Line icons in both editions, approved the studio's recommendations, and asked for tiles and light versions: "I love these icons. I just want to put them in the rounded square circle and possibly square. I approve your recommendations. We also want to do the light variations as well."

| # | Decision | Source |
|---|---|---|
| DR-07 | The 122 Civic Line icons are approved in both editions, red-black and black. The package moves to `deliverables/` once the variants below are built and checked | Dustin, October 29 |
| DR-08 | Framing option 3 (Q-15): each icon grows toward 116 of 128 units by at most 1.25 times the shared scale, so strokes run 2.68 to 3.35 units | Dustin, October 29: "I approve your recommendations" |
| DR-09 | Light editions for dark grounds: lines in Newsprint, in two versions, Newsprint with Pillar Red (for Charcoal grounds) and Newsprint alone (for Charcoal and Prussian; red never sits on Prussian) | Dustin, October 29 |
| DR-10 | Tiles for all 122 icons: square, rounded square (a continuous superellipse corner) and circle; a Charcoal tile carries the light editions and a Newsprint tile the dark editions, each in two-color and one-color, 12 tiles per icon | Dustin, October 29 |
| DR-11 | Order of work after the variants: the visual system for the atlas and systems maps, then the other open items of the direction review, then the rest of the brand finalization | Dustin, October 29 |

### Civic Line variants, studio decisions, October 29, 2026

| # | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D2-40 | Icon size in tiles: one factor per shape, square 0.68, rounded 0.66, circle 0.62, chosen on the calibration board; every icon keeps at least 21 units of margin to the tile edge | One consistent size across the three shapes; the circle cuts into the corners of wide marks, so it takes a smaller factor |
| D2-41 | The rounded square is a superellipse of exponent 5, drawn as a 96-piece spline with continuous curvature | The Phase 2 holder path had curvature jumps and concave samples |
| D2-42 | Minimum tile sizes: two-color 72, 76 and 80 px (square, rounded, circle); one-color 48, 52 and 52 px, or 72, 76 and 80 px for the 28 icons that need 48 px on their own | Below these sizes the line falls under a pixel and the red stops reading as Pillar Red (variant review M-1) |
| D2-43 | Every id inside an SVG starts with its file's own root, and every group carries `data-part` | Repeated mask ids broke CL-057's knockout when two files shared a page (variant review M-2) |
| D2-44 | In tiles, 36 icons take an optical scale from 0.94 to 1.09 with the line capped at 3.35 units, and 8 asymmetric icons move up to 3.8 units toward their light side; bare files keep ink-box centering | Narrows the size spread in tiles from 1.44 to 1.24 times (variant review S-1, S-2) |
| D2-45 | All 32 sheets share one header and footer geometry, each row's captions share one baseline, and the package text uses American spelling | Variant review S-3, S-4, S-6 |
| D2-46 | The light editions keep the dark editions' stroke | The design director found no visible gain in apparent weight from Newsprint on Charcoal |

## Atlas visual system, October 30, 2026

Dustin sent ten reference images, asked for a higher number of one-to-two-page concepts (the whole system plus one close-up each) built partly from his references and partly from scratch by a fresh agent, with civic education, visual design, data visualization and interaction experts where they add value. The studio asked eight questions and named two defaults. His answer: "yes i agree with all defaults. please proceed."

| # | Decision | Source |
|---|---|---|
| DR-12 | Twelve concepts: five grown from Dustin's boards, one from the October 28 studio pick (Loop Lines with the Field Guide), six from scratch. Each is an overview, one close-up and a strip of all 23 loops, at 1440 and 390 px | Dustin, October 30 |
| DR-13 | Like-for-like close-ups: R10 for the loop and area C for the sub-system in every concept. R8 still appears in each loop strip | Dustin, October 30 |
| DR-14 | No invented tier between area and variable. The levels are the system, an area with all its variables, a loop or variable, and a reform | Dustin, October 30 |
| DR-15 | Each loop thumbnail is the loop's own route across its concept's system map, with each step's sign and evidence texture, so loops of the same length still look different | Dustin, October 30 |

## Dustin's direction on nonpartisanship, November 6, 2026

Dustin: "I want this to be fair and nonpartisan ... I just don't want to be so focused on being nonpartisan and trying to make everything look like there are problems on both sides, but corruption and being a threat to democracy, those things are probably not balanced between Democrats and Republicans. And I don't think we should act like they are if that's in fact true ... make sure that we're not so focused on keeping everything equal and balanced that we're not unable to objectively talk about the truth and facts."

| # | Decision | Source |
|---|---|---|
| DR-93 | Nonpartisan, for Oath & Action, means one standard of evidence and one way of describing sources, actors and parties, applied to everyone. Findings are reported as the record shows them. When the evidence shows one party or official doing more of something, the copy says so, names who acted, and sizes the claim to its evidence grade (H08, W20). No piece adds, softens or drops a finding to make the sides look even, and no piece invents a counterweight. Positions Oath & Action takes are labeled as positions (DC-01) and argued from graded evidence. The editor's disclosure stays wherever a reform he works on is argued (D2-106) | Dustin, November 6 |
| DR-94 | Proposed for Dustin's approval on stage board A: DR-55's clause "the chart set is chosen so readers on each side meet a finding they would rather skip" is replaced by "Charts are chosen by the question and the evidence. Every title is read from the left and the right to check that its words are accurate and fair. A set never adds or drops a finding to even the score." Until he answers, the fix pass follows DR-93 | Studio, from DR-93 |

## Stage B round 0, engineer, November 9, 2026

### Engineer (`20261109_Stage-B-Engineer-Notes.md`)

| # | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D2-568 | The pixel-fit check is a Node script that renders in Chromium, and the drawings brief's `.py` path runs it through a thin wrapper | No SVG renderer exists for Python here, and Chromium draws icons as the guide does |
| D2-569 | The pixel-fit check rasterizes by screenshotting the SVG inline in a page at device scale 1, 2 and a supersample | A canvas keeps a non-scaling stroke at one canvas pixel at any zoom, which misstates the fifth edition's lockmark masters |
| D2-570 | Pixel-fit thresholds: thinnest stroke 1.0 device px, soft edges 95 percent, straight edges within 0.1 px of a pixel edge, gaps 1.0 device px, the same counters as drawn, and with `--weight` the main width within 0.15 px | Under one device pixel a line never reaches full ink; the soft share cannot tell a fit round drawing from an unfit one, so it fails only an all-fringe drawing |
| D2-571 | A stroke of fractional width passes with one edge on a pixel edge, a whole-pixel stroke needs both | A 1.5 px line at 16 px cannot hold both edges on whole pixels |
| D2-572 | A drawing passes a size only when it passes at 1x and at 2x | Both densities are in use |
| D2-573 | The look-alike check flags at the 99th percentile of the masters' pairs (0.913) and lists from the 95th (0.871) | The master pairs above the 99th are drawings that share a base; a utility icon that close would read as part of the topic set |

| D2-783 | A 24 px pillar icon is one `<svg>` with `<use class="oa-at-1x">` of the fit and `<use class="oa-at-2x">` of the master; a 16 px pillar icon is one `<use>` of its fit | DR-179 and D2-634 in one pattern that keeps one glyph, one box and one name |
| D2-784 | The shell switches the two classes at `min-resolution: 2dppx` | The pixel-fit figures: the master meets the line and gap floors only from 2x, and fit B keeps every counter and gap below it |

| D2-788 | A missing family folder is a note; an empty folder, an unreadable file, a second file for a symbol and a reference to no symbol are warnings, the last naming the missing folder when that is why | A folder still on its way should not stop the build; a broken one should |
| D2-789 | `forcedGlyphs` reads a `<use>` glyph's paints from its symbol's markup | A `<use>` hides its shapes in a closed shadow tree, and the masthead's glyph is now one |
| D2-790 | `sprite_check.js` compares each `<use>` with its file drawn inline in the same cell in turn, at 1x and 2x, and passes at a channel difference of 8 or less | Chromium's raster of one drawing differs by up to 29 levels between two places on a page at 2x and 128 px; in one place the two draw identically |

| # | Decision | Reason |

| D2-1137 | WCAG's words come from the Recommendation of 12 December 2024, with each Understanding page linked | The Recommendation is the normative text; the quick reference and the Understanding pages are W3C's informative companions |

| D2-1141 | "First stable version" means the first version with full support, without a flag, prefix, other name or partial mark | Counting partial support, such as popover on iOS before 18.3, would overstate what a rule can rely on |
| D2-1142 | `text-wrap: pretty` and `forced-color-adjust` count as extras, never as the only way a rule is met | MDN records no Firefox support for `pretty` and no Safari support for `forced-color-adjust` |
| D2-1143 | The masthead menu maps to APG's disclosure navigation example, not the Menu pattern | APG's example says site navigation does not use the menu role |
| D2-1144 | Status lines and the bill tracker's updates cite WAI-ARIA 1.2's `status` role and `aria-live="polite"` | APG has no pattern for a polite live region |

| D2-1433 | The results cite only checks whose results hold; the check's miscounts on the new chapters go to its owner | Honesty rule: never describe a result the run did not support |
| D2-1434 | The device script uses only basic gestures and keys, and says where they differ by version | A person with no training must be able to follow it, and gestures change between versions |
| D2-1435 | The device script asks for ten things to listen for, the same on every device | One list makes four devices' results comparable |
| D2-1436 | Numbers under ten in the chapter's prose print as words, through the generator | House rule H06 |

## Answers on board C, November 11, 2026

Dustin, on board C's one question: "do not, who cares about my work? I'm supportive of these reforms because I think they're the right things to do. All-party primaries with ranked general elections, ranked choice voting for single winner races should absolutely stay in this document." And: "I agree with all other recommendations."

| # | Decision | Source |
|---|---|---|
| DR-223 | No standing rule on examples. Reform R-01, All-party primaries with ranked general elections, and Reform R-02, Ranked-choice voting for single-winner races, stay in the guide and are treated as every other reform under one standard (DR-93): a maker may use either, or the mechanism it targets, as an example, sample or failure plate, at its real grade with its source. The studio stops moving examples off them because of the editor's own work. This supersedes the avoidance reasons of DR-99's "touches no campaign the editor works on", D2-203, D2-278, D2-958, D2-1546 and D2-1555 from today on; examples already moved stay where they are unless a maker picks R-01 or R-02 on the merits. DR-100 stands: the guide carries no disclosure | Dustin, November 11, declining the recommendation (D2-1555) |
| DR-224 | Every other open recommendation on board C stands as made: the dark field ground on Charcoal (D2-1544), the ask on its own row (D2-1545), an identity player resting on its master (D2-1548), tokens.css as the one route (D2-1549), and the token table as closed groups (D2-1557) | Dustin, November 11, "I agree with all other recommendations" |

Round 0 (the chart kit, the palette proofs and chapter 13) ends at board 1; form rounds 1 to 3 end at board 2; rounds 4 and 5 with chapter 15 end at board 3; then Gate 3. The guide stays one file. Chapter 14 may not pass 1.75 MB; chapter 13 takes 0.25 MB and chapter 15 0.60 MB | Dustin, November 11: "Gate 2 is approved as proposed" |

| D3-535 | In forced colors a kit drawing takes the system ground, Canvas, as its own background | The reviewer's recommendation: it holds in any host, the guide's stage that keeps Newsprint, the atlas site and a plain page |
| D3-536 | Beside a red point, context marks take Canvas with a CanvasText outline in forced colors, by a `:has()` rule | The point stood apart from GrayText bars by hue alone (access S10); the rule changes no drawing's markup |
| D3-537 | The zero line takes the value scale and draws only where it holds zero | The line belongs to the scale; a line placed by a number cannot know (CD RM1) |

| D3-539 | The guide check draws every kit chart and fails a shape 1.5 px or more outside its drawing or sketch | The kit's own tolerance in the browser (dataviz_check), on the page where the fault showed |
| D3-540 | `wide_px` switches at 680 px only, and the shell's query names it | A container query cannot read a variable; D3-480 fixes 680, so any other figure warns |
| D3-541 | `plate_css` prints as `@scope (#<anchor> [data-specimen])` | Every fragment's own style already takes `@scope` from the build, so the guide asks nothing new of a browser |
| D3-542 | `frame_container` stays, unused, with its self-test | It costs nothing in a page that does not use it, and a later plate with one size may need prose that follows its frame |
| D3-543 | A sketch of one drawing takes a stage of its drawing's width; an avoid pair takes a flowing stage, each half as wide as its drawing | True size, centered, with the bar the drawing's width (SK-M2, BC S7); a pair wraps where a phone cannot hold both |
| D3-544 | The notes' rule runs the figure's width; each note keeps its 38em measure | Two rules of one figure at one measure (K-S1), with the reading measure kept |
| D3-545 | Chapter 14's side list nests each group's rules and opens only the group in view once the script runs; with scripts off every group stays open | The list ran past 60 lines with no current rule in sight (ST-S1, BC S19); a reader with no script loses nothing |
| D3-546 | Where the script runs, a lookup's controls and table move into the still drawing's place, which hides; the kit's build page keeps the still drawing | One list and one count (AM1, BC S9); the still image is the export, scripts-off view and print (Rule 14.13), and its PNG needs it shown |
| D3-547 | The source check fails a line whose named years leave out a year the chart draws, reading the years outside retrieval, release, vintage and "as of" dates | It catches the production test's "fiscal 2026" under 2016 and 2026; a stricter test, an exact span, waits until every multi-year file has its template, since approved plates name the table's span over a window |
| D3-548 | Templates fill {from} and {to} with years only | The research lead put templates on annual files; months and quarters need words a year cannot give |
| D3-549 | The dots check fails a row whose two dots sit closer than a dot's radius only where its values differ by a tenth or more of the larger | A small change drawn as touching dots is honest (S04's 65 and over, 74.5 to 74.7); a hidden 54 percent rise is not (production test P01) |
| D3-550 | The dumbbell moves its scale's ends in until every row's end values fit, and keeps today's ends where they already fit | Access S2 without moving S04 (unchanged) |
| D3-551 | A kit figure's stage is a group named "specimen", with no description of its own; "interactive specimen" only where the manifest says interactive | The drawing carries the alt as its name, so a reader heard it twice (access S3); the table's disclosure is part of every figure |
| D3-552 | A drawing taller than 864 px may break inside in print; the kit marks it before print | A drawing taller than a page was pushed whole to the next page and left its title alone (access S4) |
| D3-553 | The chip's check mark takes an empty alternative text, and Escape in the filter sheet closes it and returns focus to Filters | `aria-pressed` carries the state; chapter 18's filter bar closes on Escape (access S7) |
| D3-554 | The phone drawing at a desk is drawn by the figure's own script from its own rows, hidden from assistive technology | No second copy of the data on the page (BC S3); the description and table carry the data once |
| D3-555 | Straight lines of a whole-pixel width snap to the pixel grid in the drawing context, not in each part | Every form draws its lines through `c.line`, so one rule reaches them all (BC S16 named `parts.js`; the arrow, the one part that draws a line with other shapes, snaps all three) |
| D3-556 | The chapter's basis prints once after the introduction as a Basis box | BC S18; one box where 54 repeated it, and an entry's own box marks a basis that differs |
| D3-557 | Rules 14.1 to 14.14 with the opener are measured against 212,288 bytes, and sketch entries are reserved whole at the built ones' measured words | Call 4, and the creative director's weight paragraph |
| D3-558 | A drawing made outside the kit is set in the kit's figure by a command, not by copying the kit's classes into a script | One place prints the anatomy, the bold "Source:", the credit and the table (BC M2, ED-E9) |
| D3-559 | Typography from a spec applies to words drawn from data and to text cells, never to the data or the CSV, and a replacement must not feed itself | Atlas values are copied, never edited (D3-349); the page reads its rows back from the printed table, so the setting must give the same words twice |
| D3-560 | The claims lines count the claims that carry the check date, by script | 432 of the 433 claims carry October 15, 2026 and CL1723 carries none (ST S6) |
| D3-561 | ENG-13's columns are added to the copied Phase 2 file row by row, and the script stops if a ban lacks a city reading or a reading lacks a ban | The file's first 11 columns stay byte for byte as Phase 2 left them |
